```
I have the full file. Now I'll apply the skill's checks. Let me work through the headings against the achievement test, check the body for orphaned terms, and review the two sentence fixes against style-rules.md.

Let me verify the §9 "horizon" concern by checking body usage, which I can see: §9 body uses "The horizon extends outward", "The horizon is a deliberate trade", and §10 refers back with "the autonomy horizon of §9". So "horizon" is a defined term the body and a cross-reference depend on.

Here are my findings.

## Verdict: accept with notes

The two sentence-level fixes (B) are clean. Several headings (A) still read as bare noun labels and need a verb or result; none of the necessary rewrites orphan a defined term, including §9 — see the specific guard below.

---

## 1. Headings (A) — achievement test

The achievement rule (Phase 3.3): a heading carries a verb or a result. I sorted all 13 by whether they already pass.

**Already pass (verb or result present) — leave alone:**
- `## 2. Layer 2 captures the workflow as composable skills` — verb "captures".
- `## 3. Layer 1 owns process, not procedure` — verb "owns", plus a sharp contrast.
- `## 5. Layer 2 writes signals; Layer 1 reads them` — two verbs; this is the model heading for the doc.
- `## 6. Dual-mode: the same skills by hand or unattended` — the colon tag is a label, but the predicate states the result (one skill set, two modes). Acceptable.
- `## 8. \`prompt.py\` becomes a pointer, not a script` — verb "becomes" + contrast. Strong.
- `## 10. From a one-shot CLI to a resident daemon` — no finite verb, but the "From X to Y" frame states a transition (a result-shaped trajectory). Acceptable; borderline.

**Weak — still a bare noun label. Concrete rewrites:**

1. **`## 1. Two layers, one product`** — pure noun label, no verb/result. The section's point sentence is "The work splits into two layers." Rewrite to carry that verb:
   `## 1. Split the work into two layers over one record`
   (No orphan risk: "two layers" stays in the heading.)

2. **`## 4. Phase separation: a worker per phase, not a worker per issue`** — "Phase separation" is a nominalization-label before the colon; the post-colon clause is good but still has no verb. Reverse the nominalization into the verb the body uses ("Each phase ... is its own spawn"):
   `## 4. Spawn a worker per phase, not per issue`
   (Drops the label "Phase separation"; the body never refers back to "the phase separation" as a term, so nothing is orphaned. The phrase "phase boundary" in §4/§5 stands on its own.)

3. **`## 7. All state lives in one record — the state plane`** — has a verb ("lives") but reads as a definition tag; the em-dash trails the defined-term label. It's borderline-passing but flat. Tighter, result-forward:
   `## 7. Keep all state in one record — the state plane`
   Minor. The em-dash "the state plane" is a *defined term the rest of the doc relies on* (§3, §5, §7, §10, §12 all say "the state plane"), so keep that tail — do **not** cut it. Optional fix.

4. **`## 9. The supervisor's autonomy horizon`** — **weak label, you flagged it correctly.** Pure noun phrase, no verb, no result. But here the orphan risk is real: the body uses "the horizon" three times as a back-reference ("The horizon extends outward...", "The horizon is a deliberate trade...") and §10 cross-references "the autonomy horizon of §9". A naive achievement rewrite that drops the noun (e.g. `## 9. Run further without the operator as trust grows`) would **orphan "the horizon"** — the body's "The horizon" would then have no antecedent in its own heading, and the §10 cross-ref "the autonomy horizon of §9" would point at a heading that no longer names it.
   So the rewrite must keep the term *and* add a verb/result. Recommended:
   `## 9. Extend the autonomy horizon as observability earns trust`
   This carries the verb "extend" (which the body already uses: "The horizon extends outward as observability earns more trust"), states the result (trust-gated extension), and preserves "autonomy horizon" so both the body's "the horizon" and §10's cross-ref keep their antecedent. This is the one heading where the achievement rewrite and the orphan constraint actively pull against each other; the fix resolves both.

5. **`## 11. The execution unit: a cycle or a project`** — label-colon-enumeration, no verb. The body's point is "The supervisor executes a *planned unit*." Rewrite:
   `## 11. Execute a planned unit — a cycle or a project`
   (Keeps "planned unit," "cycle," "project," all used in the body. No orphan.)

6. **`## 12. Multi-altitude review: the dual of the delivery hierarchy`** — label colon label; "dual of the delivery hierarchy" is a result-ish noun but still verbless. The body's point: "Verification rolls *upward* along the same tree." Rewrite:
   `## 12. Roll review up the delivery hierarchy, altitude by altitude`
   ("Multi-altitude review" is referenced back in §"undecided" as "The multi-altitude reviews (§12)", so that exact phrase is a cross-ref antecedent. Safer variant that preserves it: `## 12. Roll review up the tree: multi-altitude review`. Either works; the first is punchier, the second is orphan-safe. Prefer the second given the existing cross-ref.)

7. **`## The one undecided boundary crossing`** — bare noun label, no verb, and it's the trailing unnumbered section. The point: a verdict-handoff schema is a one-way door that must be decided before the correctness work starts. Rewrite:
   `## Decide the verdict-handoff schema before the correctness work`
   (States the action and the result-deadline. "Boundary crossing" isn't reused as a defined term elsewhere, so no orphan.)

---

## 2. Sentence fixes (B) — clean?

**§1 Layer 1 bullet** — "Picks up a body of planned work and spawns a worker per phase per issue, holding the guardrails and halting, reverting, resuming, or recovering as the run demands. It then records and grades each outcome. It is vendor-agnostic: ..."
- **Clean and an improvement.** Splitting the run of slashed verbs into a comma series ("halting, reverting, resuming, or recovering") fixes the stray-hyphen pileup, and breaking out "It then records and grades each outcome" as its own sentence keeps the participle stack short. Actor-as-subject holds (implicit "Layer 1" from the bold lead-in, consistent with the §2 bullet's "The intra-issue procedure...").
- **One small note, optional:** the lead clause now stacks one participial phrase ("holding the guardrails") followed by a four-verb gerund series ("halting, reverting, resuming, or recovering") under a single "and." It reads fine, but if you want it tighter: "Picks up a body of planned work and spawns a worker per phase per issue, holding the guardrails — halting, reverting, resuming, or recovering as the run demands." The em-dash signals that the four verbs *are* how it holds the guardrails, rather than reading as a second coordinate item. Not required; current version passes.

**§2 parenthetical** — "(The \"Consolidate Shaper's Lifecycle into Four Phase Skills\" project tracks this consolidation.)"
- **Clean.** The nominalization reversal worked: actor ("The ... project") is now the subject with an active verb ("tracks"), versus the old passive "Consolidation tracked by...". Passes characters-as-subjects.
- **One residue:** "tracks this consolidation" still ends on the nominalization *consolidation*, and the project's own name already contains "Consolidate." So the noun "consolidation" is doing the antecedent work for the project title — acceptable, but if you want zero nominalizations: "(The \"Consolidate Shaper's Lifecycle into Four Phase Skills\" project tracks this work.)" Trivial; the current text is fine.

---

## 3. New problems the edits introduced

- **No heading now overstates its body** — I checked each rewritten/edited heading against its section's claims. The §2 heading "captures the workflow as composable skills" is supported (body: "captured as composable skills"). No claim-without-support introduced.
- **The §1 bullet edit introduces no factual drift** — "records and grades each outcome" matches §3's "record, grade" and the doc's framing of Layer 1 as the grader. Good.
- **Watch the §1 prose body vs. the bullet** — unrelated to your edit, but flagging since it's adjacent: the opening paragraph (line 5) still uses the slashed form "halts/reverts/resumes/recovers"-style construction? No — line 5 uses "moving each piece from one step to the next," so it's consistent. The slashed verbs only survive in line 39 ("spawn, guardrails, halt, revert, resume, recover, record, grade") where they're a deliberate comma-listed inventory, not prose. Leave it.

**Net:** fix the seven weak headings above (especially §9, with the orphan-safe rewrite given), and the two sentence edits are good to keep as-is. No edits made to the file.
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/docs/images/README.md",
  "content": "# Architecture diagrams\n\nThese diagrams illustrate [`../architecture.md`](../architecture.md). The committed `.svg` files are clean, first-pass renders that display on GitHub today. They are meant to be **redrawn in the hand-drawn Excalidraw aesthetic** later; this file is the label/layout spec to redraw from, so the wording stays 1:1 with the doc.\n\n**Rule that keeps the visuals honest:** the doc is authoritative. Every label here appears verbatim in `architecture.md`. Do not introduce friendlier or invented names when redrawing — if a term needs to change, change the doc first, then the diagram.\n\n## Diagram 1 — `architecture-overview.svg` (apex visual, after the lead)\n\nA top-to-bottom flow from the operator into the supervision layer, then into one planned unit, with review rolling back up.\n\n- **operator** → **Control plane (one per machine, resident daemon).** Layer 1. Owns process lifecycle and the queue of planned units; exposes a steer API (what's running · halt · resume); watches open PRs through the review-and-merge loop and responds to PR comments (horizon behaviour — only a resident process does this).\n- Control plane **spawns one Execution-coordinator per unit in flight.** Layer 1, a tree walker. Advances work by reading artifacts (§5), never by reading inside a phase; halts on a missing or failed artifact.\n- The coordinator drives one **planned unit**, drawn as nested containers: **PROJECT ⊃ MILESTONE ⊃ TASK (one issue).**\n- Inside the task, three **phase spawns** run in sequence, each a Layer 2 skill:\n  - `exec:build` — goal: produce sliced artifacts (+ `exec:debug`, `exec:simplify`)\n  - `exec:review` — goal: apply every quality lens (fans out reviewer personas)\n  - `exec:finish` — goal: land human-readable PRs (What/Why/Focus PR · summary comment · status move)\n  - Annotate the build→review boundary: **\"the agent that produced an artifact never judges it.\"**\n- **Review altitudes** sit to the right, each aligned to its container:\n  - **Task review** — diff-bounded, before the PR merges: spec-compliance · security · reliability/resilience · code-quality · outcome.\n  - **Milestone review** — integration/coherence/acceptance · regression (outside this boundary); after child PRs merge → new work, not reverts.\n  - **Project review** — architecture review · measurable stated goals (partial — some goals deferred).\n- Edge arrows: **work fans down (decomposition)** on the left; **verification rolls up** on the right.\n- Colour key: Layer 1 = blue, Layer 2 skills = green, review = amber.\n\n## Diagram 2 — `artifact-boundary.svg` (supports §5)\n\nTwo stacked boxes with the boundary between them.\n\n- **Layer 2 — the workflow pack (skills)** on top: does the work, then leaves signals behind. **WRITES ↓.**\n- **The artifact boundary** is a dashed line carrying three artifacts: **Linear issue state · run-log fields · `.drain-handoff.json` (`pr_urls`).**\n- **Layer 1 — the supervisor** below: advances to the next issue, or halts, on whether an artifact exists. **READS ↓.**\n- Caption: Layer 1 never reads the workflow's steps — only the artifacts it leaves behind; that content-blindness lets the same Layer 2 run under any worker.\n\n## Diagram 3 — multi-altitude review (deferred)\n\nNot drawn. The overview (Diagram 1) already shows the fan-down/roll-up duality and all three review altitudes, and `architecture.md` §12 carries the per-altitude lens table. Add a dedicated diagram only if a future reader finds the roll-up idea underserved by those two.\n"
}
```

```
## Findings: SVG/doc consistency audit

Overall: **PASS** with two minor near-match notes. No correctness defects, no forbidden labels, no broken references.

### 1. Term parity — PASS (one cosmetic mismatch)
Every load-bearing diagram label appears in `architecture.md`. All explicitly-required terms confirmed present: `control plane`, `execution-coordinator`, `exec:build/review/finish`, all five task lenses, milestone lenses, project lenses, the "never judges it" line, `Linear issue state`, `run-log fields`, `.drain-handoff.json`, `pr_urls`.

- **Minor — lens spelling differs (not a missing term).** Diagram 1 renders the task lens as `reliability / resilience` (spaced, SVG line 104); the doc and README both write `reliability/resilience` (unspaced, architecture.md line 107; README line 20). Same term, different spacing. Cosmetic; flag only if you want strict 1:1 character parity.

No diagram term is absent from the prose, and no prose-required term is missing from the diagrams.

### 2. Forbidden / invented labels — PASS
Scanned both SVGs for `process manager`, `Code It`, `Review It`, `Merge It`, `*-agent-coordinator`, and `performance` (as a lens). **None present.** Diagrams correctly say "control plane" and "Execution-coordinator", and the task-review lens list ends at `outcome` with no `performance` lens.

### 3. Markdown image references — PASS
Exactly two `![alt](path)` references in architecture.md:
- Line 11 → `images/architecture-overview.svg` — descriptive multi-clause alt text.
- Line 64 → `images/artifact-boundary.svg` — descriptive multi-clause alt text.

Both paths resolve relative to `docs/`; both files exist (`/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/docs/images/architecture-overview.svg` 8.2K, `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/docs/images/artifact-boundary.svg` 3.1K). Both alt texts are non-empty and descriptive.

### 4. Diagram 1 placement — PASS
The overview image (line 11) is the first body element after the intro lead (lead paragraphs at lines 3, 5, 7, 9) and sits before `## 1. Split the work into two layers, one product` (line 13). It is not inside the lead.

- **Note on phrasing, not a defect:** the prompt calls it a "four-paragraph intro lead." The lead is a `Pre-read:` line (line 3) plus three prose paragraphs (lines 5, 7, 9). The placement intent — first body element after the lead, before `## 1.` — holds regardless.

### 5. README spec vs SVGs — PASS
Spot-checked README labels against rendered SVG `<text>` content:
- Diagram 1: control-plane bullets, coordinator description, `PROJECT ⊃ MILESTONE ⊃ TASK`, the three phase-spawn goals, the "never judges it" annotation, all three altitude lens lists, fan-down/roll-up edge labels, and the colour key (Layer 1 blue / Layer 2 green / review amber) all match.
- Diagram 2: the two layer boxes, WRITES/READS direction labels, the three boundary artifacts (`Linear issue state · run-log fields · .drain-handoff.json (pr_urls)`), and the content-blindness caption all match.
- The one spacing difference from finding #1 (`reliability/resilience`) is consistent between README and doc; only the SVG spaces it.
```

1	# Architecture
2	
3	Pre-read: [docs/vision.md](vision.md)
4	
5	Shipping a piece of agentic work is never one action — it is a sequence: code it, review it against the right standards, open the PR, make the description read well. A capable agent handles any one of these steps, one at a time, and driving each by hand holds up fine while the work is small. As the work grows more complex, that hand-driving breaks down — and what breaks reveals that the operator was holding three fused jobs together: the **state** (where the work stands), the **steps** (what runs, in what order, to what standard), and the **supervision** (moving each piece from one step to the next). Holding all three by hand carries two costs: execution quality swings with the operator's discipline on the day, and the operator must drive every transition by hand instead of planning the next work.
6	
7	So the question this architecture answers: how do you build the thing that holds the work together once — so the workflow runs the same way every time, without a person driving it — and how do the pieces of that system fit together?
8	
9	The answer is two layers over one shared record. **Layer 1 — supervision** (`drain-cycle`) drives the work and grades each result. **Layer 2 — workflow** (the Shaper pack) is the procedure itself, captured as composable skills. They meet at an **artifact boundary**: Layer 2 writes signals, Layer 1 reads them, and neither reaches inside the other. Both work off one **state plane** — a single authoritative record of where the work stands. The move that makes this real is small: `prompt.py` stops inlining the workflow and becomes a thin pointer at the workflow's front door, `exec:pickup`. The pack then owns the procedure, so any vendor's worker follows the same prose. The payoff removes both costs above — the workflow is captured once and a content-blind supervisor runs it unattended, so execution is consistent on every run instead of varying by the day, and the operator is needed only to point the system at planned work and to step in when a run halts.
10	
11	![Architecture overview: the operator points the control plane at planned work; a control plane (one per machine) spawns an execution-coordinator per unit; the coordinator drives a planned unit (project ⊃ milestone ⊃ task) whose task runs three Layer 2 phase spawns — exec:build, exec:review, exec:finish — while review rolls up the task, milestone, and project altitudes. Work fans down; verification rolls up.](images/architecture-overview.svg)
12	
13	## 1. Split the work into two layers, one product
14	
15	The work splits into two layers:
16	
17	- **Layer 1 — supervision (`drain-cycle`).** Picks up a body of planned work and spawns a worker per phase per issue, holding the guardrails — halting, reverting, resuming, or recovering as the run demands. It then records and grades each outcome. It is vendor-agnostic: each worker is a `claude -p` (or any equivalent) subprocess.
18	- **Layer 2 — workflow (the Shaper pack).** The intra-issue procedure, captured as composable skills: how a piece of work goes from picked-up to merged PR. This is the knowledge that used to live in the operator's head, written down once.
19	
20	The vision's three pieces map onto this split: "one place for state" is the state plane (§7), "the steps become skills" is Layer 2, and "the supervision becomes a supervisor" is Layer 1.
21	
22	## 2. Layer 2 captures the workflow as composable skills
23	
24	The pack carries two verb namespaces, one per half of the lifecycle:
25	
26	- **Planning — `shape:*`.** Four front-door skills: `shape:idea`, `shape:project`, `shape:design`, `shape:delivery`. (The "Consolidate Shaper's Lifecycle into Four Phase Skills" project tracks this work.)
27	
28	- **Execution — `exec:*`.** The intra-issue graph, named delegations only, no inlined procedure:
29	  
30	  ```
31	  exec:pickup → exec:breakdown → exec:build → (exec:debug | exec:simplify)
32	              → exec:review → exec:verify → exec:finish
33	  ```
34	  
35	  `exec:pickup` is the front door; `exec:review` fans out reviewer personas; `exec:finish` is Graphite-first with a plain-git fallback and produces the trail artefacts (What/Why/Focus PR body, review-summary comment, Linear status move). The execution namespace is pinned by ADR 0004 / the Shaper `execution-workflow` design doc; that doc is authoritative for the graph and the handoff contract.
36	
37	Every step is also a standalone skill, so a human can run any one by hand. A **handoff envelope** carries the issue's acceptance criteria from pickup through to review, so the spec-compliance persona can grade *built-the-wrong-thing*, not just *built-it-badly*.
38	
39	## 3. Layer 1 owns process, not procedure
40	
```

1	# Linear initiative — multi-agent collaboration for correctness
2	
3	> **Now a facet of the north-star.** The system architecture this initiative assumed is consolidated in [`docs/architecture.md`](architecture.md) (serving the [vision](vision.md)). Under that design this initiative is reframed as the **Layer-1 (supervision) enforcement + recording + grading** project — its six-role *skill-building* is overtaken by the pack's `exec:*` skills, already delivered by the "Issues drain end-to-end on first-party skills" project. Read the architecture first; treat the role/flow detail below as historical context for the recording/enforcement/grading work that survives. The surviving scope is stated in §0 below.
4	
5	## 0 Surviving scope (Layer-1 only)
6	
7	This initiative now builds **only drain-cycle's Layer-1 work** — enforcement, recording, and grading. The Layer-2 skills it originally proposed are delivered:
8	
9	- **Roles 1–5 (Task Shaper, Implementer, Outcome Verifier, PR Preparer, PR Responder)** are the pack's `exec:*` skills — `exec:pickup`/`exec:breakdown`, `exec:build`, `exec:verify`, `exec:review`/`exec:pr-prepare`, `exec:pr-respond` — shipped by the "Issues drain end-to-end on first-party skills" project. Not rebuilt here.
10	- **What survives in drain-cycle:**
11	  1. Run-log recording fields: `outcome_verdict`, `prep_verdict`, `responder_runs[]` (M1).
12	  2. The **verifier-gated Done** contract: a ticket reaches Done only with a recorded `outcome_verdict` (KR2) — this supersedes design decision §1's agent-self-asserts-Done.
13	  3. Grading: `drain-cycle grade-draft` / `grade --flow=verify` (M5).
14	
15	The role/flow narrative below is retained as historical context. Where it says drain-cycle *builds* a `/shape:*` skill, read it as "the pack provides the `exec:*` skill; drain-cycle records and grades its artifact." The artifact boundary is [`docs/architecture.md`](architecture.md) §2.
16	
17	---
18	
19	**Six agent-driven roles take a Linear ticket from intake to merged PR — so hard tickets don't silently ship with missing AC.**
20	
21	**The bet.** drain-cycle today drains a Linear cycle unattended — one ticket, one worker, one diff. The next step: six agent-driven roles operating end-to-end on the same ticket — task-shaping before code, outcome verification before Done, structured PR preparation, and autonomous handling of human review feedback. Same operator, same tickets, less manual shepherding.
22	
23	**What we'll know in 5 hard tickets.** Of the next 5 hard tickets drained under the new flow, the bet is ≥ 3 ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits. Otherwise the kill condition triggers (§5) and contract enforcement reverts.
24	
25	---
26	
27	## 1 The problem and the bet
28	
29	**Why this matters beyond drain-cycle.** drain-cycle is the batch driver for a growing toolbox of composable skills and MCPs. Each new skill compounds across both modes of use — at the keyboard during interactive work, and inside a worker session during a drain. This initiative ships the next four skills plus two CLI subcommands the toolbox needs to take a hard Linear ticket from intake to a merged PR, plus the durable contracts (run-log fields, Linear-issue dual-write, OTel attrs) that make their outputs inspectable afterwards.
30	
31	drain-cycle evolves from "drain a Linear cycle unattended" toward a **UNIX-philosophy dispatcher for composable skill primitives** — Linear tickets as the work queue, skills+MCPs as the small tools, and `drain-cycle` itself as the batch-mode shell that composes them. The operator (you) drives the *why* (initiative goal + KRs) and the *what* (outcome-focused Linear issue); agents handle the *how* through a defined set of skills. The same skills run manually at the keyboard or automatically inside a worker session — design decision §10's symlink makes this true.
32	
33	The operator failure mode this initiative addresses: **complex tickets that can't be sliced further** where the worker forgets details, under-verifies, or submits an unstructured PR — followed by human review comments piling up while the operator manually re-invokes the agent each round. Three concrete failures today:
34	
35	1. **Silent Done on hard tickets.** Complex unsliceable tickets get drained; the worker self-asserts Done; the diff misses AC; the operator finds out at PR review and fixes by hand. *(e.g., a ticket asks for "halt the drain if Linear label resolution returns ambiguous matches"; the worker ships the label-parse change but never adds the halt logic; the diff passes self-review because nothing it touches asserts the halt-on-ambiguity contract.)* Left unaddressed, the unattended-drain promise breaks on the tickets where it matters most.
36	2. **Unstructured PR submission.** PRs land without What/Why/Focus guidance; the reviewer has to guess what to look at, or the operator manually rewrites the body. *(e.g., a multi-file refactor lands with a subject line and no body; the reviewer reads the diff blind to find out whether the risky bit is in `labels.py` or `worker.py`.)* Left unaddressed, PR review burden stays entirely on the human — auto-mergeable changes get the same scrutiny as risky ones, and review throughput suffers.
37	3. **Stalled feedback loops.** Human review comments accumulate on stacked PRs; the operator manually re-invokes claude each round. *(e.g., a stacked PR gets three rounds of review comments across two days; each round the operator opens the worktree, re-launches claude, and shepherds the fix manually.)* Left unaddressed, the agent ecosystem stops at code-submission time instead of running end-to-end through merge.
38	
39	The bet: six agent-driven roles end-to-end, each backed by a skill (or CLI subcommand) fitting the UNIX-philosophy frame. A drained ticket flows through the pipeline below; the per-role detail table follows.
40	
41	```
42	┌─ Operator (you) ──────────────────────────────────────────┐
43	│  throughout · defines why (KRs) + what (Linear ticket)    │
44	└──────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
45	                               ▼
46	┌─ Task Shaper ─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
47	│  before code · /shape:task                                │
48	│  → enriched AC + sizing decision + per-stack task list    │
49	└──────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
50	                               ▼
51	┌─ Implementer ─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
52	│  middle · 6 existing build/test/review/commit skills      │
53	│  → code + tests + in-session code review                  │
54	└──────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
55	                               ▼
56	┌─ Outcome Verifier ────────────────────────────────────────┐
57	│  after impl · /shape:verify-implementation                │
58	│  → pass/fail verdict in run log (gates Done)              │
59	└──────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
60	                               ▼
61	┌─ PR Preparer ─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
62	│  before submit · /shape:pr-prepare                        │
63	│  → What/Why/Focus body OR auto-merge label                │
64	└──────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
65	                               ▼
66	┌─ PR Responder ────────────────────────────────────────────┐
67	│  on review feedback · /shape:pr-respond                   │
68	│  (operator launches via `drain-cycle pr-feedback`)        │
69	│  → fix lands on stack · comment IDs tracked               │
70	└──────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
71	                               ▼
72	┌─ Grader ──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
73	│  post-drain · `drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>`           │
74	│  → ~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md                       │
75	│    (status: draft → operator confirms)                    │
76	└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
77	```
78	
79	| # | Role | Phase | Skill / CLI | Status |
80	|---|---|---|---|---|
81	| 0 | **Operator** (you) | Throughout | Operator-side skill table; defines *why* + *what* | — |
82	| 1 | **Task Shaper** | Before code | `exec:pickup`/`exec:breakdown` (pack) | delivered by A |
83	| 2 | **Implementer** | Middle | `exec:build` + `exec:debug`/`exec:simplify` (pack) | delivered by A |
84	| 3 | **Outcome Verifier** | After implementation, before Done | `exec:verify` (pack) | delivered by A |
85	| 4 | **PR Preparer** | After Outcome Verifier passes, before PR submission | `exec:review`/`exec:pr-prepare` (pack) | delivered by A |
86	| 5 | **PR Responder** | On new human review comments (operator-launched polling loop) | `exec:pr-respond` (pack), launched via `drain-cycle pr-feedback` subcommand | delivered by A |
87	| 6 | **Grader** (post-drain) | After worker session finishes | `drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>` CLI subcommand | [—] |
88	
89	"Delivered by A" = the "Issues drain end-to-end on first-party skills" project shipped the `exec:*` skill in the pack. This initiative's remaining work is drain-cycle's Layer-1 enforcement/recording/grading around those artifacts (§0). Roles 1 and 3 are a **complementary pair**: `exec:pickup` defines the outcome-shaped task list at the start; `exec:verify` validates the outcome at the end. Roles 4 and 5 are the **PR-side pair**. Role 6 is operator-confirm scaffolding for grading the initiative against its KRs.
90	
91	**If the initiative is built:**
92	
93	- **Hard tickets clear AC before they land.** The Outcome Verifier sees the diff before Done is recorded; the verdict goes to the run log; halt-on-fail leaves the worktree intact for operator inspection rather than producing a silent partial.
94	- **PR review starts with a structured map.** Reviewers open a PR and see What / Why / Focus from the start; auto-mergeable changes route past human review correctly; risky changes get the human attention they earn. Review throughput rises because reviewers no longer parse intent from the diff alone.
95	- **Human review feedback closes its own loop.** The operator launches `drain-cycle pr-feedback`; new review comments get addressed on the right stack node; comment IDs are tracked to prevent double-handling; the loop continues until the operator stops it. The agent ecosystem runs end-to-end through merge rather than stopping at submit-time.
96	
97	**Constraints:**
98	
99	- Subscription auth only (Claude Code / Codex CLI subprocess) — no API-key models. GitHub webhooks are free, but the agent can't run inside GitHub Actions; agent work happens on the operator's machine.
100	- Correctness > throughput. No parallel drains. No throughput-focused changes.
101	
102	## 2 Affected repos
103	
104	- **`drain-cycle`** — primary, and now the *only* repo this initiative builds in. Layer-1 work: run-log schema extensions (`outcome_verdict`, `prep_verdict`, `responder_runs[]`), the `verify` label mechanism, the verifier-gated Done contract (supersedes design decision §1), CLI subcommands (`grade-draft`, `grade --flow=verify`), and OTel attribute additions.
105	- **`agent-skills-shaper`** — the `exec:*` skills that fill roles 1–5 are already delivered by the "Issues drain end-to-end on first-party skills" project; no new skill work belongs to this initiative.
106	
107	## 3 Goal and Key results
108	
109	The KRs are plain English — a non-pack-author should grade each in 30 seconds.
110	
111	### 3.0 Goal
112	
113	**For** the operator running drain-cycle on outcome-focused Linear tickets, **we want** six agent-driven roles that take work from ticket to merged PR — task-shaping before code, outcome verification before Done, structured PR preparation, and autonomous response to human review comments — **so that** complex tickets don't silently land with missing AC, PRs are reviewable without guesswork, and human review feedback doesn't create a manual re-invocation tax.
114	
115	### 3.1 KR1 (stretch) — Of the next 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow, ≥ 3 ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits *(bet)*
116	
117	- **plain-English version:** when I run drain-cycle on a hard ticket I couldn't break into smaller pieces, six agent roles take it from intake to merged PR. The bet: this catches enough real problems and structures the PRs well enough that most merge cleanly without me pushing fix commits.
118	- **baseline:** 0 of 0 — none of the six roles exist as a connected flow today.
119	- **target:** ≥ 3 of the next 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits.
120	- **measured over:** the first 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow (~2 cycles at current cadence).
121	- **how we'll grade it:** per-ticket grade markdown at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` (auto-drafted by `drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>`, operator confirms `draft` → `confirmed`); `drain-cycle grade --flow=verify` reports the rolled-up pass-rate across the window.
122	
123	### 3.2 KR2 (commit) — Every ticket reaching Done in Linear under the new flow has a recorded Outcome Verifier verdict in the run log; zero exceptions *(brake)*
124	
125	- **plain-English version:** the worker no longer marks its own ticket Done. The contract: a ticket using the new flow reaches Done only if the run log shows the Outcome Verifier ran and produced a verdict. Protects against the failure mode where the agent thinks it's finished but missed half the AC.
126	- **baseline:** silent self-assertion currently possible (per design decision §1); the count of past silent Dones is unknown because the recording fields don't exist yet.
127	- **target:** 0 tickets reach Done in Linear under the new flow without an `outcome_verdict` recorded in the run log.
128	- **measured over:** every cycle running the new flow, for the initiative's duration (cap at 10 cycles if the initiative runs long).
129	- **how we'll grade it:** `drain-cycle grade` fails any entry where `flow == "verify"` AND `final_linear_state == "completed"` AND `outcome_verdict` is absent. Exit code ≠ 0 on any violation. One violation in any cycle fails the KR — this is a brake, not a rate.
130	
131	### 3.3 KR3 (commit) — Every PR submitted under the new flow has the structured What / Why / Focus body, or is correctly routed to auto-merge *(brake)*
132	
133	- **plain-English version:** the PR Preparer either writes a structured What / Why / Focus body and routes the PR for human review, or correctly identifies the PR as auto-mergeable (no critical focus items) and routes it to auto-merge. Either way, every PR ships with a documented routing decision and a reviewable body.
134	- **baseline:** today, PRs from drained tickets carry whatever body the worker produced — typically a one-line subject and no review guidance.
135	- **target:** 0 PRs submitted under the new flow without either (a) structured What/Why/Focus body OR (b) auto-merge label with PR Preparer reasoning attached.
136	- **measured over:** every PR submitted under the new flow, for the initiative's duration.
137	- **how we'll grade it:** PR Preparer writes its decision to a `prep_verdict` field on the run-log entry; `drain-cycle grade` fails any verify-flow entry where `prep_verdict` is absent or where the verdict says "structured body" but the PR body is empty or single-line.
138	
139	The PR Responder (role 5) gets no KR of its own — KR1 captures it structurally (clean-merge rate degrades if the responder doesn't address review feedback).
140	
141	## 4 Appetite
142	
143	**~18–22 issues across five milestones.** Each milestone ends with a review gate — the operator reviews progress and either continues to the next milestone or pauses for a reshape/kill decision if something clear has surfaced. The gate default is review-and-continue, not stop-the-line. Whole-initiative kill conditions are separate (§5).
144	
145	### M1 — Recording infrastructure
146	
147	Build the data layer first, behaviour later.
148	
149	- `verify` Linear label parser added alongside `repo:` / `model:` resolution in `labels.py`.
150	- Run-log schema fields: `flow` (string, e.g. `"verify"`, or `null` for default flow), `outcome_verdict` (object: `{result, findings[], invoked_at}` or `null`), `prep_verdict` (object: `{result, route, reasoning}` or `null`), `responder_runs[]` (array of objects: `{comment_ids[], invoked_at, result}` — appended each time the responder runs).
151	- Matching attributes on the `drain.issue` OTel span.
152	- `docs/design-decisions.md` §1 supersession: write the successor decision explaining the verifier-gated Done contract; mark §1 as superseded.
153	
154	**Gate:** an end-to-end `verify`-labelled ticket drains; new fields populate (all `null` since no roles are wired yet); existing behaviour unchanged.
155	
156	### M2 — Worker-internal loop (Task Shaper, Outcome Verifier, halt path)
157	
158	- **Task Shaper** (`/shape:task`): per-ticket task-shaping skill that takes a Linear issue and produces an enriched AC checklist + sizing decision (single stack vs N stacks) + per-stack task list. Distinct from `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` (whole-project / design-doc shaping); the two coexist in the toolbox at different scopes. The precise interface is resolved in design decision §17 (`docs/design-decisions.md`): `/shape:task` runs inside the worker session at implementation time; `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` stays at design-doc scope and is not extended to emit per-ticket artefacts.
159	- **Where the sizing decision lands**: dual-write. (a) Fed into the implementer in-session as context; (b) appended to the **Linear issue body** so future resumes / pickups carry the full context without re-running the Task Shaper. The Linear write is the durable artefact.
160	- **Implementer integration**: worker session pulls the Task Shaper output as context; no other implementer changes.
161	- **Outcome Verifier** (`/shape:verify-implementation`): new skill. Inputs: ticket body + Task Shaper output + diff + run-log. Output: structured verdict (pass / fail + findings) written to `outcome_verdict`.
162	- **Halt-on-fail path**: extends decision §8 / §9 machinery — on Outcome Verifier fail, leave worktree, halt cycle, name the breach in `cycle_halt_reason`.
163	
164	**Gate:** 3 verify-labelled hard tickets drained; verdicts recorded; halt path exercised at least once on a deliberate failure (e.g. a synthetic ticket whose AC the implementer can't satisfy).
165	
166	### M3 — PR-shape gate (PR Preparer)
167	
168	- **PR Preparer** (`/shape:pr-prepare`): new skill. Reads stack PRs via Graphite MCP; writes What / Why / Focus body on each; decides auto-merge vs human-review per the carve-out rule (small + no human review needed → auto-merge; otherwise → human-review).
169	- **Auto-merge mechanism**: Graphite-native (`gt submit --merge-when-ready`-equivalent).
170	- Records the decision to `prep_verdict` on the run-log entry.
171	
172	**Gate:** 3 stacks emitted, all with structured What / Why / Focus bodies or auto-merge labels. PR-body assertion in `drain-cycle grade` passes on all three.
173	
174	### M4 — PR-feedback loop (PR Responder)
175	
176	- **PR Responder** (`/shape:pr-respond`): new skill. Reads new review comments on stack PRs via Graphite MCP; makes the fix in the right worktree; pushes to the stack; appends to `responder_runs[]`.
177	- **Trigger model**: a separate operator-launched subcommand — `drain-cycle pr-feedback`. The operator launches it explicitly; the command then runs as a polling loop, addressing PR comments as they arrive, until the operator stops it. Not invoked implicitly at drain-start, not a GitHub Action, not a boot-time background daemon. A foreground polling agent whose lifetime the operator owns.
178	- **Idempotency**: each comment ID tracked in `responder_runs[].comment_ids[]` so the same comment isn't addressed twice across `pr-feedback` invocations.
179	
180	**Gate:** one real PR with operator-left review feedback gets addressed end-to-end by the responder; the fix lands on the stack; the run-log shows the responder run.
181	
182	### M5 — Grading + cost + close-out
183	
184	- **Per-ticket grade template** at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` with checklist + `status:` field.
185	- **`drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>` CLI subcommand**: one-shot grading prompt. Runs automatically on drain completion (writes `status: draft`); also runs manually for retro-fill on past tickets.
186	- **`drain-cycle grade --flow=verify` subcommand**: reads `confirmed` grade files, reports pass-rate across a ticket window, warns about un-confirmed drafts.
187	- **Silent-Done detection** (KR2 grader): exit nonzero on any `flow == "verify"` AND `final_linear_state == "completed"` AND `outcome_verdict == null` entry.
188	- **Cost-impact handling**: measure per-ticket token multiplier under the new flow; add `verify`-flow-specific overrides in `limits.yml` if cycle cap pressure surfaces.
189	- **Post-launch review**: write a Linear comment on each KR's status at the end of the initiative window; either ship cleanly, pause, or kill.
190	
191	**Gate:** KR grading runs end-to-end; one of {ship cleanly, pause, kill} is decided and recorded.
192	
193	## 5 Kill condition
194	
195	**Primary kill (whole-initiative):** after running the new flow on 5+ hard tickets across 2 cycles, the Outcome Verifier produces zero true-positive findings (every halt was a false positive, and verifier-passed Dones don't visibly improve PR-review outcomes vs default-flow drains).
196	
197	**Secondary concerns (watched at each milestone's review gate, not auto-kills):**
198	
199	- M1 — schema additions break run-log readers or downstream tooling.
200	- M3 — PR Preparer's bodies don't measurably reduce reviewer cognitive load (signal: the operator subjectively prefers the raw worker-produced PRs).
201	- M4 — PR Responder either (a) requires operator intervention to address comments cleanly >50% of the time, or (b) drops or misses comments in practice.
202	
203	Any of these surfacing at a milestone review prompts a discussion (continue / reshape / kill), but the default is continue. Only the primary kill above is automatic.
204	
205	**Action on kill:** revert the contract enforcement (the `verify` label becomes a no-op or is removed); keep the recording fields — they stay in run log + OTel because they're cheap and useful for future investigation; record the negative result as a new decision in `docs/design-decisions.md`.
206	
207	## 6 Out of scope (explicitly)
208	
209	- **Parallel drains.** Decision §8's serial-drain stance unchanged.
210	- **Replacing Graphite.** The PR-side work assumes the Graphite stack flow; switching PR-management tools is a separate decision.
211	- **Multi-operator / multi-tenant workflows.** Single-user product per `AGENTS.md` ("Python, single-user, personal product").
212	
```

```
=== DD §1 title + supersede status ===
## 1. The spawned agent updates Linear itself

> **Superseded-by (pending).** The "Multi-agent collaboration for correctness" Layer-1 project replaces self-asserted Done with a **verifier-gated Done** contract: a ticket reaches Done only with a recorded `outcome_verdict` (its KR2). The supervisor records the verdict; the agent no longer asserts success unobserved. Until that lands, the decision below stands.

The orchestrator does **not** poll Linear and write status. The spawned `claude -p` session is told, in its prompt, to move its issue to Done on completion. The orchestrator only reads Linear after the session exits, to decide whether to advance or halt.

**Alternative considered.** Orchestrator-owned status: the parent polls Linear, transitions states, owns the lifecycle. This is more conventional and easier to reason about.

**Why the agent-self-update path.** The orchestrator can only observe *process exit*, not *task success*. A Claude session may exit 0 having done nothing useful, or exit non-zero having actually shipped — exit code is a poor proxy for "the issue is Done." Letting the agent assert Done in Linear forces it to make an explicit, observable claim about its own outcome, which is exactly the artefact we need to grade KR1 and trigger the kill condition. If this pattern proves unreliable, that's the initiative's kill condition firing — not a bug to paper over.
```
